Even so, he was made to feel something like a discrepancy from time to time.

That he'd rather hide it, if possible, was also Daiki's undisguised truth.

"So, you've got something on your mind?"

His friend asked again. Apparently he was genuinely worried.

Had he looked that troubled? Tilting his head, Daiki answered with gratitude in his voice.

"Just a bit of wondering what to do from here."

"Ahh, career stuff, then."

"Mm, more or less. Broadly speaking, yes."

It wasn't accurate, but framing it that way was less trouble.

He could hardly consult anyone about having befriended a magus and being dragged into an incident.

Still less when what troubled him wasn't the incident itself.

"Even so, you're planning to go on to university, right?"

"Well, I intend to get into a university. I don't have anywhere in particular I'm aiming for."

That had been Daiki's thinking before he went to the other world.

Nothing very admirable: the easy, difficult daydream of vaguely aiming for a good university and vaguely wanting a job at a good company.

And what of his present self, by contrast?

Daiki didn't know.

The simple matter of just going back to how he'd been was, for some reason, beyond him.

Somewhere there was always a hard-to-articulate wrongness clinging to him.

—Is it really all right for me to stay like this?

Isn't there something else, something I want to do, something I want to aim for—?

Is it all right for me to be in this place — at all?

That sense of wrongness had, for a year now, refused to be wiped away.

"Well, I figure everyone's like that at this point... hey, Daiki."

His friend, who had been listening with narrowed eyes, glanced away toward the outside of the classroom.

"Something wrong?"

"No... isn't it kind of noisy?"

A school after classes is by definition a noisy place. There was nothing strange about it.

But now that he mentioned it, something in the air from the corridor side was certainly different from usual.

"Hm...?"

Daiki turned his eyes that way too.

Which was when he noticed: for some reason every student in the corridor had stopped walking and was looking in a single direction.

As though someone extremely conspicuous were walking toward them from that way.

"—Ah, here it is. Class 2-5."

And then a voice came flying into the classroom.

Along with that familiar voice, a familiar face popped in, peering into the room.

It felt as though his hearing and his sight had been punched — not by force, but by freshness.

"There you are. Morning, Daiki. I've come to pick you up!"

Her face brightening at once, the girl he'd met last night — the magus — gave Daiki a little wave.

The same black uniform as yesterday. A visitor's pass hung from her chest, pointlessly correctly.

"..................Oki... -san?"

"Yep. Come on, let's go. Up, up."

There, as though it were nothing at all, stood Narumi Oki.

Every gaze in the classroom, and in the corridor beyond it, stabbed blatantly into Daiki.

A considerable crack tore across his ordinary life — so Daiki would later recall it.

It was an «Assault».

"Why... are you here— ah, no, ahh."

He had all sorts of things he wanted to ask, and no time for any of them.

Daiki hurriedly gathered his things and stood. His friend, right beside him, grabbed his shoulder.

And, of course, asked this:

"...What's going on?"

"I'd like to know myself... Sorry, I don't want to explain it here."

"No, but, hey — that uniform, she's a middle-schooler, isn't she...?"

"How can you tell just from the uniform— ...no, wait, seriously?"

—Breaking news! Oki! The middle-schooler hypothesis!!

That was its own kind of confusing, and there was no time for it!

"Sorry, I'll give you the full explanation at a class reunion in about five years, so, later!"

"—That's way too much later!"

"It's fine, life goes by in a flash!"

Firing off a remark he didn't understand himself, Daiki fled into the corridor.

Watching this, Oki tilted her head, round-eyed and blank, and said,

"Huh, sorry. You didn't have to hurry that much."

"You're saying that wasn't an act designed to make me hurry?!"

—Is she an airhead? Did she pull this off out of pure obliviousness?

If so, that was troublesome in its own way. Was she really unaware that she stood out?

Turning all sorts of things over in his head, Daiki nonetheless seized Oki's hand.

"Eh, wawa, Daiki?"

"Let's just get out of here, quickly. Come on."

Allowing no objection, Daiki pulled Oki along and out of the school building.

—In the days that followed, the rumour that Kurosu of Class 2-5 was dating a middle-schooler would be whispered for some seventy-five days; but since Daiki was an unremarkable sort, it reportedly didn't cause much trouble.

1-06 "The Former Hero's Everyday Life" — 2
-------------------

"How old are you?!"

Having escaped the school and got far enough away to be out of sight, Daiki — who had swallowed and swallowed a great many things he wanted to object to — let that slip at the very last.

Probably because it was the least important of them, and the one that bothered him most.

"Huh, out of nowhere...?"

Oki, who had been dragged along without complaint, looked up at him, taken aback by the first words out of his mouth the moment they stopped.

Somewhat winded, Daiki caught his breath.

"Haah... no, sorry. Asking a woman her age is rather bad form. So, how old?"

"That was not bad form at all... well, I don't mind. I'm fifteen this year."

"...Third-year middle school... two years younger..."

Not that it ought to constitute a problem, and in fact that ought not to be the issue in the first place.

Even so, Daiki was surprised that she was younger than he'd thought.

Though now that it had been said, it wasn't as if she *didn't* look like a middle-schooler.

In fact — whether because he knew now, or for some other reason — she looked like nothing but a middle-schooler.

Thin and small and breakable.

"What. Don't tell me you're going to treat me like a child at this stage?"

Oki said it with a thin smile. Daiki shook his head.

"No, I've no intention of changing my attitude."

"Huh, really?"

"Middle-schoolers and high-schoolers are both children."

"...Maybe so."

Oki laughed, her shoulders shaking lightly.

The image the words carried was probably different for Daiki and for Oki, but he didn't correct her.

"More to the point. Why did you come all the way to the school?"

Daiki asked it again. That was going to be awkward going forward.

But without any particular air of gravity, Oki answered easily.

"Why? The question is why you thought I wouldn't. Obviously I'd come to collect you."

"...Obviously, huh..."

"Well, I suppose we could have met somewhere. At the gate, say. But, you know, we forgot to exchange contact details yesterday. So I figured while I was at it I'd check out the inside too."

"While you were at it..."

"It's necessary, isn't it. I have to know the area around the person I'm guarding. It'd be no laughing matter if I got complacent and you were targeted. What are you going to do if someone comes for you while you're at school?"

Put like that, it began to sound like sound reasoning.

Daiki knew nothing of the reality of these people called magi.

With no source of information but Oki, he had, on some level, resolved not to go against her policies as a general rule.

"...All right, I understand. That's fine, then. Though it would help if you'd make this a one-time thing."

"That's fine, but... why does it bother you so much?"

Oki seemed genuinely not to understand that part.

After thinking about it a little, Daiki said,

"Try thinking about it the other way round — what would you think? If I showed up at your school."

"I'd think you shouldn't be wandering around on your own, because it's dangerous. I'd want to know where you are at all times."

"That's not what I mean. Setting this whole business aside."

"...Then I don't see the problem?"

Oki said it flatly.

She doesn't see it... Daiki massaged his temples.

"Huh, was something wrong with it? I said I was from the Kagomeboshi Middle student council and they let me right in. They even told me your class."

"Huh. So you're in student council, Oki."

That was somehow unexpected. It seemed at odds with the title of magus.

But at Daiki's mildly impressed remark, Oki stuck out her tongue playfully with a "teehee."

"As if. There's no way I'd be doing extracurriculars conscientiously. I don't have that kind of time."

"Then in what sense did they 'let you right in'... That's a flat-out lie."

"Now, now. They say a lie is a free-for-all, don't they?"

"They don't. And if they did, whoever said it would be a liar."

"Naturally — it's a convenient principle for liars. Of course only a liar would say it."

At Oki saying it with a perfectly straight face, Daiki had no choice but to press his temples again.

"...Still, I see. So that's the Kagomeboshi Middle uniform."

Kagomeboshi Middle — that was, if he remembered right, a fairly upmarket private young ladies' school in the city.

Was it at odds with her image, or did it suit her in a certain way? For Daiki it was a fine line.

"That's right. I'll have you know my grades are good."

Oki said it with a touch of pride. Then she went on,

"But look at you — Miyashiro High is top class in the prefecture. You can study."

"...Sorry to spoil the compliment, but my grades tanked because of the other-world excursion."

"Ahh..."

"No, anyway, never mind that. Back to the subject: you'd be in trouble too, wouldn't you, if I showed up at your school out of nowhere and your friends saw and started rumours? That's all I mean."

Daiki said it as though talking her through it. But Oki, still perfectly unbothered:

"No, I don't have friends at school or anything."

"...That's—"

"Ah, it's fine, it's fine. I'm a magus, so of course I don't. That's just how it is."

There wasn't any air of putting on a brave face.

Only the manner of someone stating the plainly obvious.

"I do have to make some kind of accommodation with the surface world, of course. Which is why I keep my grades respectable. But in the end it's not as though there's anyone there I'll keep dealing with later in life. I get by at school without standing out, without making noise, without making waves. Nobody's going to care about me just because you came to visit."

"..."

That can't be true, Daiki thought.

Even if Oki was, as she said, a plain and unremarkable student at school. If something happened, attention would follow, and it was impossible that literally nobody would give it a thought.

Even Daiki, still in rehabilitation from life in another world, could work out that much easily.

—But.

"Hm. Still, I see. So it means you're actually surprisingly popular at school, Daiki. Ah, well, that's not surprising. But I hadn't thought that far. I won't do it again, all right?"

Oki, who had presumably lived her whole life in this world, did not understand it.

"Well... if you get it, that's fine."

Daiki shook his head. The dissonance he'd felt in her since last night was gradually becoming visible.

Come to think of it, it was unnatural from the start.

She had certainly said this city had a mutual-aid relationship among magi. Which meant there were magi in this city besides Oki.

She'd also said she'd taken the job from someone and was chasing an outside magus, and that someone was presumably older than Oki — an adult.

—Why is a girl not yet even fifteen chasing him alone?

"Right. Never mind that — Daiki! ...Are you listening?"

Oki called to Daiki as he brooded.

"Ah— sorry. What?"

"'What,' nothing. Never mind that, I have something to ask you too."

"Something to ask?"

Was it about the job, or about the other world again?

Daiki nodded and waited for the question. Oki fixed him with a flat glare.

"Your arm. ...Why is it still like that?"

"Ah, this?"

Saying it, Daiki waved his left arm loosely about.

It was Oki who hastily stopped him, in a panic.

"Wai—?! What are you doing, that's much too rough! And why haven't you been to a hospital?!"

Oki pointed it out with something like anger showing.

At this question it was Daiki's turn to look puzzled.

"...How do you know I haven't? I might have gone this morning."

"If you had you'd at least be in a cast... There's a crack in the bone, you know?"

The direct cause had been Oki's fist, which was exactly why she was worried.

Since she had used magecraft, the force easily reached the level of a professional baseball player's full swing with a metal bat.

Daiki's total unconcern was the strange part — and he, too, looked startled.

"**Huh?** **From something like this?**"

"......Excuse me?"

"That's awkward. Right, if it's a fracture then of course... I wasn't thinking either."

Oki couldn't understand what Daiki was saying.

The immobilisation and anaesthesia she'd applied last night had long since been released. And yet.

"Well, it's fine. Something like this heals if I sleep it off. It doesn't even hurt as much as I expected."

"...Are you serious?"

"It's hard to tell my parents I broke a bone. And it costs money to treat. Injuries at this level I'm used to from my experience in the other world, so it's honestly not a problem. It isn't life-threatening. If anything, considering what's coming, it's more of a problem to wrap it in bandages and lose mobility."

He was serious.

Oki could tell that Daiki was serious.

—**She thought it was frightening.**

She was made to think it.

A person who was supposed to be a magus had been made to feel fear toward a person who was supposed to be an ordinary civilian.

Did Daiki have any awareness of how abnormal that was?

He didn't, presumably.

Under some misapprehension, he went on demonstrating to Oki how fine he was.

"Yeah, see, look. It feels a lot better than yesterday. I can move it fine."

And in fact Daiki rotated the forearm of his broken arm around and around, as if there were truly no damage at all.

He then went so far as to punch the injured spot — midway between elbow and wrist — with his right hand, casually.

"—What are you doing?!"

"Ah. No, I mean, hitting it does hurt normally, doesn't it."

"O-obviously it does, what are you even saying?! A-are you an idiot...?!"

"You're exaggerating, obviously I did it lightly. Even I know how to hold back."

"'Hold back,' he says."

"Besides, I drank milk this morning. I got my calcium."

And what was that supposed to do. Oki glared at Daiki, then let out a breath and shook her head.

"...All right, I understand, so stop doing that. I don't want the injury getting worse."

Oki touched Daiki's arm gently and slowly lowered it.

Then, just in case, she looked around to confirm nobody was watching, and once she'd checked that there were no people about, she activated the same magecraft as the night before.

Jet-black threads like shadows that reflected no light instantly wound around Daiki's arm.

Oki wasn't a doctor, so she didn't know in detail how much good the immobilisation did.

"I'll leave the pain this time. In any case, don't overdo it, Daiki."

"Mm, thanks, Oki. This stuff is amazing, isn't it — even when I hit it the shock doesn't get thro—"

"I said! Don't! Hit! Your broken arm! —Idiot!!"

"Eh? R... right. It's honestly not that much of a problem... and I'm not toughing it out, either."

Daiki's manner suggested he was merely stating a rational judgement that it was fine.

And in fact the injury might be lighter than she'd thought, Oki considered. She considered it — but that didn't stop this excessively careless handling from worrying her. It was worse for Oki's heart than for the patient's.

In yesterday's fight, Oki had assessed Daiki as calm.

But perhaps that was a little off.

It wasn't calm — it was beginning to seem like a simple absence of any sense of danger.

She'd been right to press him into the contract, forceful or not.

He was too precarious to watch.

"You really were fighting in another world... weren't you?"

He'd done well to survive it, Oki thought.

Daiki, mildly shocked to be doubted at this stage, said,

"That's exactly why. I can tell the state of my own injuries because of it — sort of."

"You seem like you'd do nothing but reckless things."

"Ahaha..."

Daiki laughed it off. —He couldn't deny it.

It was simply that it had been necessary to survive. That was all there was to it, as far as Daiki was concerned.

"Right! Never mind that — shall we start today's work?"

To get the conversation back on track, Oki turned to face him again and brightened her tone.

Daiki nodded and put a question to the magus.

"Sure. What should I do?"

"Mm — that said, there's nothing much to do until night. Shall we kill some time?"

"...I'd just got motivated."

"Can't be helped. Magi move at night as a rule. So once the sun sets we'll make a patrol and look for traces. If there's movement, either I'll notice it — or you'll notice it first."

"And then it's time to charge in. Right, understood."

At Daiki nodding, Oki drove in a nail.

"Let me be clear: your job ends at finding him. Don't lay a hand on anything."

"I know. I don't know how it'll play out, but I'm not going to be useful."

"...As long as you know. If it comes to it, you run, all right? I mean it."

"Did I suddenly lose all your confidence...? It's fine, I'll follow instructions. An incompetent ally is worse than an enemy. My job is to stay out of your way. Right?"

"You understand that part perfectly well, and yet... You're out of joint somehow, Daiki."

"Am I? ...Well, maybe. You might be right."

Daiki gave a light, wry smile.

He had never once thought it a shame to have lost his power.

But that was because he'd assumed it was a power of no use whatsoever in modern society. Which was why Daiki didn't cling to his old strength.

And so.

What about now, by contrast?

How did he regard the powerlessness that left him with no choice but to entrust everything to a girl younger than himself—?

Daiki didn't know.

"...Well, shall we go, then. Where do we kill time until nightfall?"

At Daiki's question, Oki blinked, eyes going wide.

"Come to think of it, I hadn't thought about it. ...Is there anywhere you want to go?"

"No, nowhere in particular for me either. Let's walk around and find something."

Saying so, Daiki set off walking.

To Oki, pattering along behind him, he turned and said:

"It's a date, after all. If you like, allow me to escort you."

For Daiki this was strictly a joke, an invitation within the bounds of form.

He'd meant it as the courtesy of an older party, someone who had, after all, gained years' worth of extra experience in another world.

"...........................................Fweh?"

Unfortunately, when it came to turning a friendless — which is to say entirely inexperienced with the opposite sex — loner magus girl bright red,

it was a phrase with what could fairly be called excessive firepower.

"Ah, ah, a— d-d, da, dada, daaa—?!"

"Sorry, sorry, Oki. That's on me. Red, you're red. Your face. Or not your face — your eyes. The Mystic Eye."

"Ahh, no, um— uh, um? I didn't mean it like that, I mean, um...!"

"I know, I know. It was a joke. My fault. I had no delicacy. Forgive me."

"Ah, a j-joke? R-right, of course, obviously. Ahaha..."

"...Er."

"That's not it, right, obviously... it's not like you'd mean it with someone like me... Right. Yeah."

"Oki."

"Ah, s-sorry. I was just curious. Uh, um — what is it, Daiki?"

"—If I'll do, then let's go on that date after all."

Oki detonated.

1-07 "The Former Hero's Everyday Life" — 3
-------------------

Daiki Kurosu had at least enough backbone to act like a man for a girl who had looked so disappointed.

It might be his imagination. But watching her since yesterday, he had the sense that Oki sometimes let slip an air of being lonely for company.

—Which was why Daiki was thinking that he could at least keep her company himself.

"......Something about this doesn't sit right with me."

Muttering, Oki sat down across from him and looked up at him with dissatisfaction.

Right now Daiki and Oki were visiting a café in front of the station. The place, called «Nodokaya», was a hidden gem Daiki had frequented now and then before. He hadn't been for a while, but if he was going to recommend somewhere, here was best.

—For some reason, Oki had been struck speechless on entering the shop, which was worth wondering about.

But right now, more than that:

"Why are you exactly the same as always... mrgh. And I was nervous..."

Oki slips into polite speech sometimes, Daiki thought irrelevantly, and answered.

"Why am I being glared at?"

"It's that attitude! ...Daiki, are you secretly quite the playboy? Are you a gentleman of popularity?"

"'Gentleman of'... Unfortunately, I've never had a single girlfriend in my life."

"Not even in the other world?"

"Not even in the other world."

"Was there no one you were interested in?"

"..........I plead the fifth."

"There was! There was, I knew it! See! That's why you're so unfazed!"

What the "see!" referred to was unclear, but Oki was glaring at him resentfully.

Apparently the fact that Daiki was too much his usual self on what was nominally a date did not sit well with her.

"Mmm, how to put it. That sort of thing was part of a Hero's job, you might say."

By way of soothing her, Daiki began to relate one of his old memories.

Calling it an unpleasant memory would be an overstatement, but it was one of his war stories.

"Mostly I was just travelling with my companions, and in that sense it was an easy life. But now and then, in the big cities, there'd be socialising with the noble classes, and that was a bit..."

"Nobles... huh. So the other world had an aristocracy."

That part apparently interested her, because Oki asked it with her mood restored.

Or rather, she probably hadn't actually been angry in the first place.

"Pretty much what you'd imagine. But I was completely useless at socialising — negotiating, really — with those important people, so my companions exempted me."

"Ahh... I feel like I can sort of see that. And?"

"Right. So instead I was told, 'You go and handle the young nobles,' and I ended up surrounded by noble sons and daughters... well, all of those people. It was rough."

"...Meaning?"

Oki didn't follow, so Daiki narrowed his eyes and explained.

That had been a period thoroughly ill-suited to him.

"Teaching swordsmanship to the noble sons and telling them travel stories was still fine. My swordplay wasn't the kind of thing you could teach anyone, and it was embarrassing that they liked it better when I deliberately made my exploits sound flashy... but well, that side was easier."

"Uh-huh...?"

"The problem was being surrounded by the noble daughters, young ladies of good family. That was hard going. Sitting around some confection I couldn't taste, in a beautiful garden, with 'Lord Hero, oh, Lord Hero' on all sides, and every time it was talk of romance, or outright advances... and every time I'd deflect it with a smile."

"Eep."

"Basically I was performing 'the supremely gentlemanly, upstanding hero' for all I was worth. That's what it amounts to. Providing sheltered daughters with a safe bit of playing with fire. A Hero's work."

"Huhh... so that's what Heroes do. That's unexpected. That's just being a host."

Not entirely wrong, Daiki thought.

He knew perfectly well that he wasn't the type anyone would call handsome, so it had come with plenty of stress.

"Very popular, aren't you, Daiki."

Oki laughed teasingly. Daiki made a face like he'd bitten a bitter bug and shook his head unhappily.

"Don't joke. I couldn't lay a hand on anyone even by accident — that causes its own kind of trouble."

"Ah, is that so?"

"Apparently that's how it works. The girls who understood that and toyed with me for fun were actually easier. The genuinely sheltered ones who didn't understand were — let's stop talking about this."

"You're stopping at the most interesting part..."

Oki itched to press him on it, but Daiki's face had gone genuinely pale, so she let it go.

The mystery was solved, so she'd call it good.

Before entering this place, she'd gone around a few shops with Daiki, and he had been oddly practised at escorting her — gentlemanly to a degree that verged on excessive.

It left Oki feeling both embarrassed and mildly put out, but knowing it was all part of the experience he'd cultivated in another world, she could accept it.

Though his sparkling smile had been frightening.

"But then wouldn't you be popular over here too?"

At Oki tilting her head, Daiki waved a hand.

"No, no. Noble-class methods don't work on ordinary people on Earth. You'd just put them off. In fact, I put *myself* off. Listening to myself come out with 'Would you care for more tea, my lady.'"

"Ahaha..."

—It worked on me rather well, but I'll keep that to myself.

Oki resolved as much. I am not such an easily won-over woman, thank you.

"Never mind that — I want to hear about you, Oki."

When the conversation reached a lull, Daiki sipped his coffee once and said it.

"—Hyeh?"

Oki's heart jumped at the ambush.

Daiki, not knowing that, piled on more words.

"The culprit in this case. From the way you talked yesterday, you've got some idea who he is, right?"

"...Ah. In *that* sense..."

"Hm? Was there another?"

"Nothing whatsoever. I was not expecting anything. ...Ahem."

Daiki caught on that something had been glossed over, but that aside.

Oki straightened her posture, nodded once, and fixed her eyes on Daiki's.

"I suppose it's fine to tell you. On the understanding that hearing it won't make you do anything reckless."

"...Please do."

"The magus who's intruded into this city this time. His name is Kūya Watarai."

"You know his name?"

At Daiki's surprise, Oki shook her head slightly.

"'That's about all I know' would be more accurate."

If she knew his name, he'd have thought that was a fair way into his personal details.

Daiki wondered, but since Oki's explanation continued he didn't ask. If it mattered, she'd say so.

"Age, late twenties; height about one hundred and ninety centimetres. Quite tall and well built, but he doesn't look like he trains. I do have a photograph, though it's coarse. Want to see?"

Daiki nodded, so Oki took out her phone, brought up the photo and handed it over.

A magus with a smartphone — that was its own odd sensation. Thinking this, he looked at the picture: a crowd of people, apparently cropped from security camera footage.

"The long-haired man in the middle there is him."

"...Huh."

He was indeed tall, and long-haired. He'd stand out fairly easily.

For all Oki's description, his build looked healthy enough, yet somehow there was a sickly, high-strung impression about him. Eyes sharp as a blade, and the dark rings around them, strengthened that impression.

"In terms of lineage he's second generation. His parent happened to be blessed with a talent for magecraft and an environment to cultivate it, and the child inherited it directly. Which means there's almost no magical information on him. —Summoning-type is the prediction."

"...I see."

He said so, but Daiki hadn't really followed that chain of explanation.

Being told 'summoning-type' didn't let him picture what it could concretely do.

Applying other-world equivalents wouldn't be meaningful, and would if anything breed prejudice.

"His reason for entering the city is currently unknown. Some kind of magical experiment is the presumed objective."

"I don't really follow, but... is that a normal sort of thing to happen?"

"The opposite; it's very hard to credit. There's a large shrine nearby, and this city is certainly not bad ground magically speaking... but Japan has any number of far superior spiritual sites."

"...I don't quite get it."

Daiki tilted his head, so Oki broke it down.

"Well, the point is that there certainly are places where magecraft is easier to work, and this is one of them — or at least you could argue as much — but with people like me making our base on this land, it isn't good enough ground to be worth making enemies of all of us for. And having enemies here is simply an obstruction, isn't it."

"That's true. It feels like you could do an experiment at your own house — am I off the mark?"

"No, exactly right. Precisely so. Because it's hard to believe this man has the ability to make use of the land's magical energy. A magus can't necessarily exploit spiritual ground, whatever kind it is. And if it's the same anywhere, then obviously you do it in your own territory. His reason for going out of his way to invade someone else's is a mystery."

Saying this, Oki propped an elbow on the table and made a strange face.

"Oki?"

"Nah... it really is a genuine mystery. Why on earth did he come all the way to Saitama, this man. I can't make sense of the reason at all..."

"Right... how about, he didn't know someone like you was here?"

At Daiki voicing his idea, Oki turned narrowed eyes on him.

He might have said something quite stupid.

"...Sorry. No, magi are new to me, so it just came out. That's not it, is it."

But at Daiki starting to shrink, Oki's eyes went wide.

"No — that was actually a rather good point just now."

"Huh, I was right?"

"Ah, no, you weren't right, exactly."

"...Oh. I see..."

"But I didn't have that perspective. So in that case the question becomes—"

Oki dropped her gaze as if thinking deeply.

Something appeared to have snagged.

So as not to interrupt, Daiki decided to keep his mouth shut too.

—He looked around the interior again.

It occurred to him that Oki might have known this place too. He had that sort of feeling about it.

Being down a slightly narrow alley, the shop had an unhurried air despite its prime location by the station — a hideaway café whose atmosphere he liked. He used to come often.

One reason he'd stopped was, of course, being sent to another world.

And apart from that, there was one more—

"..."

Was it because he'd let himself think about it?

At a timing he wanted to curse for it, at the end of his line of sight — the shop's door opened.

1-08 "The Former Hero's Everyday Life" — 4
-------------------

Seeing the figure who appeared in the shop, Daiki froze for just an instant.

Naturally he managed to reboot immediately, and reflexively returned his gaze to where it had been.

His first piece of luck was that his own seat was on the aisle side.

If he faced forward with his face turned away, a customer passing behind him was unlikely to notice him.

The second piece of luck was that the new arrivals were a pair. Both wore the uniform of Miyashiro High, the same as Daiki's, but he only knew one of them.

They were being shown to a table, and if her attention was on her companion, the chance of her noticing him would drop further still.

And the third piece of luck was that Oki, his own companion, was currently lost in thought.

Which meant the chance of his name being suddenly called — and of that being overheard — was also somewhat reduced.

"————"

And so Daiki devoted himself to erasing his own presence.

Naturally Daiki could not vanish as if by magic, or divert other people's awareness.

What he could do was kill sound.

Keeping his breathing, the faint rustle of cloth as he moved, every trace he generated, to a minimum.

It was one of the techniques Daiki had brought back from the other world, though whether it meant anything here was doubtful.

Lurking in a blind spot from the start was one thing, but he was in plain view. However much he killed his presence, there was a limit.

Which was exactly why, if he was going to act, it had to be at maximum speed.

Unfortunately the new pair were shown, of all places, to the table right next to his.

He wanted to curse that misfortune, but with the luck he'd had, call it even.

"Oh... I think this might be my first time in a place like this. I'm always at chains."

One of the customers at the next table spoke.

She did appear to be a Miyashiro High student, but unfortunately Daiki didn't recognise her. Small; not that that told him her year. He only had the impression of someone cheerful.

"It is a little hard to walk into, isn't it."

The other girl answered her.

This was the one Daiki knew.

A quiet-natured girl, in contrast to her lively companion.

"I have a hard time introducing friends to it myself. But I do recommend it."

"Heheh. Getting brought to Yuika-chan's regular haunt — I'm a lucky woman."

—In that instant, Daiki rose smoothly to his feet.

It was a flowing, beautiful motion. Perceiving everything around him while giving no sign that he was doing so, moving soundlessly through the gaps in others' attention — refined body control.

Had its application been something other than picking up the bill and going to the register, it would have been a skill deserving of unreserved praise.

So natural was it that even Oki, sitting directly opposite, failed to notice that Daiki had stood. He rose without so much as the sound of a chair pushed back.

He stood, and then he walked.

That was all it amounted to, and having done that much, Daiki headed for the register.

Arriving at the counter, he raised a hand to call a member of staff.

"Excuse me, could I settle up early?"

He paid for both of them together.

The result was something like a big-hearted secret treat, but internally all he wanted was to flee the shop as fast as possible before he was found. The gap between the two was appalling.

Still, inwardly Daiki was striking a victory pose.

His body had moved better than he'd expected. Favouring his left arm was unavoidable, but everything else was in full working order.

He didn't have the physical ability he once had, but the sense of body control was coming back.

—The strength training I started after coming back is paying off... nice.

Rejoicing over something slightly beside the point, he took his receipt and turned to go back to his seat. And—

"................"

The girl who had just come in was looking at him with a stiffened face.

He had been spotted, thoroughly, plainly, obviously, in the most ordinary way possible.

—Naturally, however well Daiki killed sound, he hadn't actually disappeared.

If a gaze happened to fall on him, he'd be noticed. Obviously.

Erasing only sound was meaningless. The other senses catch people perfectly well.

Daiki's valiant effort had been, contrary to his own satisfaction, futile flailing.

"...What's wrong, Yuika-chi?"

The friend sitting across from her called her name.

The girl called Yuika started, hurriedly turned back to her, and waved a hand.

"Ah, ah— no, nothing!"

"Huh? But you were staring at that person just—"

"I-I'm going to the bathroom for a second!"

Saying so, the girl stood and ran off into the shop's washroom.

Her lively-looking friend cocked her head in bafflement and turned her eyes on Daiki.

"..."

Daiki looked away and went back to his seat.

Oki had, understandably, raised her head; he said to her,

"Oki, shall we head out?"

"Huh? Ah, yes — no, but, what?"

Unable to grasp the situation, Oki was confused.

As if to exploit the opening, the girl at the next table addressed him.

"Um! Er, excuse me. Do you have a moment?"

He hadn't imagined he'd be spoken to.

A troublesome development. Even so, Daiki answered with the least abrasive expression he could manage.

"Yes?"

"Ah— er, that uniform is Miyashiro's, isn't it. Are you, by any chance, an acquaintance of Yuika's?"

A gaze that couldn't quite hide its suspicion pierced straight through him.

"...And you are?"

"...A friend. Of Yuika's."

It was a careful answer — or rather, a wary one. Daiki felt somehow like laughing.

It made him want to be relieved on her behalf: she has a good friend.

So that was what he chose to answer.

"We just went to the same primary and middle schools. So I know of her, that's all."

"The same school—"

"Sorry, we're leaving. Goodbye, then."

Having said only that, Daiki turned his eyes back to Oki.

Apologising inwardly to her as she stood up, head tilted in puzzlement.

"I've already settled the bill."

"Whuh?! But, um... that's, er, thank you very much...?"

Bringing along Oki — who for some reason had gone red and shrunk into herself — Daiki left the shop.

For a while they walked in silence. Not toward anywhere in particular.

Before long, Oki, having regained her usual tone, asked him,

"...What on earth was that about?"

"Ah... right. No, sorry. Leaving the shop like that for my own reasons."

"That's fine, but... is it something I shouldn't ask about?"

For a moment he was tempted to take advantage of her consideration.

But he thought better of it; that wouldn't be fair. He was the one who'd caused the trouble.

"That pair who sat next to us. One of them is someone I know."

"The one who spoke to you... didn't seem like it."

"Right, I don't know her. I may have done her a disservice."

"...And?"

"The other one — Yuika Asatsuma. The one with the long black hair. She was a childhood friend."

It had started with nothing more than their houses being close by.

He didn't remember the occasion, but as he recalled it, by the lower years of primary school they had already been close.

A composed, mature-natured girl; they'd simply got on well.

—Until he was sent to another world.

"I did think there was an awkward air about it."

"...Even you could tell that, huh."

"Oh-ho? What might that mean. What does that mean, please? I should like an explanation."

"Ah, sorry, that was bad. I wanted to be sarcastic for no reason. Forgive me."

She *was* a childhood friend — put in the past tense like that, she'd gather the rest even without having seen it.

Daiki shook his head once and put his mental state back in order. Then he went on.

"I said I'd been to another world, right. Since I came back — she's been avoiding me, somehow."

"...Didn't you say you'd never told anyone about that?"

"That's right. Which is why I don't understand the reason. ...Genuinely, before I knew it, I was hated."

—It got to me a bit.

Daiki murmured it, and to Oki he looked terribly lonely.

"There's nothing I can think of. So I've considered all sorts of reasons, but—"

"...Huh, but. She hasn't realised you went to another world, right?"

"Who knows. Maybe she did. I've considered the possibility."

"Th-there's no way—"

"She's always been clever, with sharp instincts. Even if she couldn't imagine another world, it might be that she noticed I was somehow different from before."

Even the family he lived with presumably didn't imagine such a thing.

But if it was her — if it was Yuika — Daiki somehow felt it wouldn't be strange for her to have sensed it.

"Or maybe there was simply a cause before I was sent over. Maybe I did something to make her hate me beforehand, and I just forgot during the journey."

"B-but... in that case, if you talked it through properly—"

Oki said it in a flustered sort of way.

It was unusual — unexpected, rather.

He'd thought she would say something like, don't let it bother you.

—No, Oki wouldn't say that.

Feeling a little lighter, Daiki answered her:

"It's impossible."

"...Why...? I mean, you... you were close, weren't you?"

"I meant to apologise at first too. If I'd done something to upset her, I wanted to apologise."

"————"

Oki realised it too. Of course — Daiki would certainly have done that.

And if that same Daiki now fled at merely being in the same shop as her, then—

"So I went to see her, wanting to make up... and that was when she said it to my face."

"...Said what?"

"—'Just having you near me makes me feel ill.' With a face like she was about to collapse on the spot."

So ever since then Daiki had made every effort never to exist near her.

Staying out of her sight as far as possible, trying to erase «Daiki Kurosu» from Yuika Asatsuma's world entirely.

"What is that. That's far too one-sided... it isn't something for you to worry about."

Oki caught her breath in sorrow for just a moment, and then, in the next, spoke in anger.

Those were words like her, and a kindness like her, Daiki thought.

"Well, it's fine. If Yuika — if she says that, there'll be a reason that fits. In that sense I must have done something, so maybe it isn't fine. But at this rate I can't use «Nodokaya» anymore. I'd assumed she'd stopped going too."

"...Do you really need to be that one-sidedly considerate?"

"I know her. She wouldn't hurt someone for no reason. Absolutely not."

Daiki said it with such conviction that Oki couldn't find a second thing to say.

Which was precisely why.

She could only imagine how it felt to be hated by someone you trusted that much.

"...Were you going out?"

What she asked instead was that — the sort of thing better left unasked.

Daiki let out a thin smile and, shoulders shaking, said,

"Who knows. People assumed so sometimes, but it was never put into words. Not once."

"...That means—"

"But now I don't know. Maybe she hates me because she never liked me to begin with, not once. Maybe she never thought of me as a childhood friend at all. If that's the correct answer, then I think the course of action I'm taking is correct too."

"Wha—"

Daiki's words were self-abasing to the point of excess. Even Oki thought that was going too far.

Only — the true intent behind what Daiki said at that moment did not come from the self-abasement Oki imagined.

Quite simply, he thought it might be a fact, and was building his words to match.

Perhaps Yuika Asatsuma had never once liked Daiki Kurosu.

Perhaps Yuika Asatsuma had never once thought of Daiki Kurosu as a childhood friend.

—**Perhaps this Earth had been that sort of world from the very beginning**—

A year since returning from the other world.

A sense of wrongness that had never once disappeared, and the conjecture that answered it.

And if that was the correct answer — then truly, where in the world was he?

"No, Daiki, I really don't think you should hold back that much— ...Daiki?"

Oki opened her mouth to object, and right in front of her, Daiki pressed a hand to his mouth and stopped walking.

As if he'd caught himself halfway through crumpling, Daiki stumbled and doubled over.

His colour was bad.

"...I feel... sick... I might throw up."

Daiki murmured it.

"Wait, you don't have to take it that—"

"No. Oki, no, that's not it. —**I feel sick**. **That kind of sick.**"

"Daiki...?"

"**It's the same sensation**... **as last night**."

"—! Then—!"

At those words Oki understood as well.

—The magus had begun to move.

1-09 "The Former Hero, and the Magus" — 1
-------------------

—He really did notice it before I did...

Inwardly, Narumi Oki set her magus's mind to work.

The time was now a little past six in the evening. Far too early to be called night; the shopping district was still crowded with people.

As an hour for a magus to start moving, it was conspicuously early.

Daiki's pace as he led the way was by no means fast — if anything it was slower than an ordinary walk.

His colour was visibly bad, and now and then he even made as if to retch.

She didn't want to push him, but this guidance was bound by the contract. He would have to endure it.

"This... way... ah, damn. This is a lot rougher than yesterday..."

"...Mm."

With heavy steps, Daiki went on.

But Oki still couldn't sense any trace of magical energy.

—Which was exactly what made Daiki's noticing it abnormal.

She had considered the possibility. That was why she'd made the contract, and if Daiki had in fact noticed ahead of her, that was something to be glad of.

But even so, the surprise remained.

Oki had currently been delegated a portion of the magical administrator authority over this whole district.

She hadn't confirmed whether Daiki remembered, but Oki was pursuing the intruder strictly as a proxy; she was not this city's magical administrator. That title belonged to a different magus.

Normally, this kind of partial delegation of administrator authority was something very close to impossible.

It wasn't unheard of for a stand-in to be appointed when the administrating magus was absent, but ordinarily one did not take custody of the administrator authority in the process.

It was possible solely because of the innate characteristic Oki possessed.

So at present Oki was able to exercise a portion of the administrator's authority, which included, among other things, a pseudo-surveillance right over the entire municipality.

It wasn't that she could view photographs or footage like a camera, but she could pick up the signs of suspicious magecraft being worked. That was how she'd learned the location last night.

**And yet that magical-energy perception had still registered nothing at all.**

It was borrowed authority in the end, so of course it was plausible that its sensitivity was worse than it should be.

Still, if she couldn't detect magecraft being activated at this close a range, the remaining possibilities weren't many.

One was a false report — Daiki's mistake, with nobody using magecraft at all. Since entertaining such a possibility at this stage was absurd, Oki had already ruled it out.

That left the possibility that the intruder was slipping past the detection barrier by some means.

"..."

Oki recalled the theory Daiki had voiced in the shop earlier — that *he hadn't known there was a practitioner in this city to get in his way*.

To get ahead of the point: the possibility that the other side was unaware of their existence was extremely low.

The reason was simple: Oki still hadn't managed to pin down the movements of the intruding magus, Kūya Watarai.

Which is to say, the other party had chosen the option of «hiding».

—Which meant he was handling things with the possibility of being searched for by a magus factored in.

He had been caught by their detection exactly once, at the moment of intrusion. The administrator — currently away — had picked that up and entrusted the response to Oki.

Oki didn't know the detailed principles behind it, but an outside magus merely entering would not make the administration barrier operate. It appeared to be a system that functioned only against «a magus requiring a response».

The formula was so advanced it exceeded Oki's understanding — it wasn't at a level a modern magus could casually reproduce — but knowing that this was how it worked was enough.

In any case, taking that into account, there was a possibility that Kūya Watarai had had no notion of hiding until he came to this city.

That was the reasoning Daiki's remark had sparked.

Though in the end he was managing to cope, so perhaps the thought was largely irrelevant—

"We're almost... there..."

Having gone in the direction away from the station, Daiki said it once they were near a shrine.

Oki raised her head too.

A large avenue leading to the precinct. A major road ran close by, with cars and people passing back and forth along it, and yet beyond the approach there was no sign of a single human being.

That was abnormal.

"...Found it. A barrier's up."

Oki activated her Mystic Eye and said it, looking at the approach.

Daiki, by contrast, couldn't sense the faintest trace of this so-called barrier.

Whatever had been true in the other world, on Earth Daiki possessed no perception of magical energy. Which was frustrating in its own way.

"A barrier at a shrine... that's insane. Does he fear nothing? Is he an idiot...?"

At Oki's faintly exasperated tone, Daiki asked,

"...Is that strange, magically speaking?"

"Depends on the objective... But having too much meaning attached to a place is fundamentally a frightening thing, I'd say. The magecraft you use gets dragged toward the land no matter what. Barrier magecraft especially — if you fail to control it, you can quite literally end up in the next world. I wouldn't want to try it lightly."

"Dragged... what does that mean?"

"How should I put it... Say I use magecraft to kindle a fire. Used ordinarily, that means *a fire was kindled*, and nothing more. But if I used the same magecraft at a shrine, it might, for instance, take on the character of *imibi* — unclean fire. Even if I have no such intent, the magecraft that activates gets carried along by the land. In practice I think there are far more parameters than that, but either way, an ordinary person can't possibly account for all of them."

"...I see."

So Daiki said, though honestly he had understood essentially none of what Oki meant.

The sense was: I probably wouldn't understand it even if you explained, but I get that odd things happen.

"Well, inverted, if you can control it completely you don't get dragged, and then you can unilaterally exploit the strong power of a place like a shrine... but I think that's a cheap payoff for the risk."

"I don't really follow, but... er, Watarai, was it? It doesn't suit his aims?"

"Put that way, we don't know Watarai's aims to begin with. But if that were the aim, I think he'd have been doing it at the shrine from the start rather than in some back alley."

"...Practice, maybe?"

"He could practise at the shrine, couldn't he."

"Maybe he thought he'd be interfered with if he went straight to the shrine."

"Then he should have come here after practising at home. That's far too seat-of-the-pants."

"Reasonable. It doesn't fit the logic... which means."

"Ah, I see. —You want to say **it might be a trap**."

Daiki nodded and glanced sideways at Oki.

"Is my thinking off the mark, from where you stand?"

"No, I think it's possible. The other side knows he's being chased, and — above all."

Oki turned her gaze on Daiki as well.

The meaning was obvious.

"Unlike yesterday, today you're with me."

If the opposing magus — Kūya Watarai — understood that too.

The odds were low, Oki judged.

But the possibility that Daiki's presence had been exposed was not zero either.

"—Let's go."

Before Oki could reach an answer, Daiki said it, flatly.

And conversely, that was exactly what made Oki hesitate over her answer. Was that all right?

"By rights I'd want you to wait here. You could even go home now, if you like."

That had been the original plan.

Get Daiki tucked away somewhere safe at once. But,

"...If this is a trap, and they know I'm here—"

"Then even the decision to part at the barrier's entrance might be something Watarai has steered us into. Is that it."

There was enough validity in Daiki's remark that she couldn't dismiss it as impossible.

With a difficult expression, Oki closed her eyes.

But when she opened them again, she had made up her mind.

"All right. —Let's go together. Up to the last possible moment, at least."

In fact it was true that keeping him close made him easier to protect.

By her calculations, Oki had indeed considered bringing Daiki along to the very end. In her rational judgement as a magus.

Not only as a radar.

He could serve as bait on a hook, or as material for negotiation.

And if it came to it, as a hostage.

Methods for making use of the asset that was Daiki, means of exploiting him to the fullest — Narumi Oki the magus had envisaged them all.

Turn a contract over and it means little more than: **do whatever you like with him, so long as he doesn't die**.

She knew all that — and yet.

—**Not a chance in hell.**

"Well, don't worry. If it comes to it I'll at least manage to run..."

At Oki's silence, Daiki said it with a smile, whatever he was thinking.

Oki smiled faintly, then shook her head and answered.

"You can't tell me not to worry with a face that pale. Our positions are reversed. I'm the one who's supposed to say *I'll protect you no matter what, so don't worry*."

"I trust you. If I do die, I'll refrain from haunting you."

"Idiot. ...We only met yesterday. Do resent me. And don't talk about dying."

"There are things I can do and things I can't. In both senses."

"...Honest to goodness, idiot."

Having insulted Daiki twice, Oki faced forward.

She was the idiot. She had simply not wanted Daiki to see through to the shadowed places in her.

—Unbecoming conduct for a magus.

That wouldn't do. Narumi Oki reminded herself that she was, above all, a magus.

"Follow me. Stay alert — you're good at that sort of thing, aren't you."

"Mm, yeah... leave it to me. I've fought monsters in darkness where I couldn't see a thing."

"Far too good at it... though I suppose I'm used to it by now."

"Ah, well. At first I got by on instinct, but a lot of them were nasty. The type that kills its presence perfectly was especially rough."

"How did you defeat something like that...?"

"...I don't want to say, because you'd definitely be put off."

"Pardon?"

"Well, look. If you stop it moving, the sword lands whether you can see or not. That's the story. Mealtimes are recommended, though you shouldn't imitate it. Lucky for me that monsters aren't fussy about taste."

"I have no idea what you're saying... All right, enough idle chat. Let's go carefully."

It was presumably some kind of other-world joke, and since she couldn't make it out, she let it go.

In silence, Oki and Daiki advanced up the approach.

Other than the total absence of any human presence, Daiki at least could perceive nothing abnormal.

He came here for the first shrine visit of the year every year; at New Year the place was always packed with stalls and crowds. Knowing that made the approach without a trace of anyone on it eerier than usual.

Meanwhile, for some reason, the further he went the more the sickness eased, slightly.

—Normally you'd expect to feel worse the closer you got...

It was the exact opposite sensation.

He didn't understand it, and since getting closer didn't actually make him feel *good*, it was a net loss that he had to go at all. Nothing decent about it.

Before long, without much time passing, they came up on the precinct.

"Ugh... ah, gh."

Right in front of the great torii, Daiki felt a tremendous wave of sickness.

But it was different in character from before.

Where the earlier sensation had been like being pulled by something, this one resembled physiological revulsion. The intuition that something unpleasant lay ahead.

"...At this level, even you can tell, I see."

Knowing the cause, presumably, Oki said it.

"This... feeling? It's different from before..."

"This is the trace of magical energy. It's quite dense here... different barriers mixed together, making a bad job of it. Magical energy is harmful to human beings, so getting close affects your condition."

"I see... by the way, didn't you pour that into my left arm?"

"What's the thing that works as an anaesthetic in small doses?"

"That's poison, isn't it..."

He'd have appreciated hearing that beforehand.

At least in the other world he had no bad memories of magical energy. Was it a different thing?

"Right — this is as far as we go. Wait here for me to come back, Daiki."

Oki said it while gazing toward the depths of the shrine.

Daiki honestly felt revulsion at so much as looking that way. Magi were apparently unaffected.

"This place is safe in a sense, anyway. Nobody's going to come in, for one thing."

"That may be... true... ah, but I'm getting used to it a bit."

"It's probably better not to get used to magical energy... Well, the culprit is unquestionably beyond this point, so no harm will reach you. I'll pop in and stop him."

"...Yeah, understood. I'll wait quietly for you to come back."

"Right — thirty minutes, let's say."

Oki said it.

At Daiki's narrowed eyes, the girl continued.

"Thirty minutes. If I'm not back by then, assume I'm dead and run."

"...Oki."

"I don't intend to lose. I'm quite strong, you know. But I can't promise 'absolutely.'"

"If it looks hopeless, you can just run away, can't you..."

"Ahaha, that's a possible pattern too. Anyway, thirty minutes minimum. Even if I lose, I'll hold out that long with my life on the line, so if I'm not back after that, run and go to the Nodokaya."

"The Nodokaya — why?"

"No time to explain. Just give my name and have them get in contact with that shop's master somehow — he's away at the moment. If you do that, they'll take care of the rest."

"...All right."

It was very hard to accept. The latter instruction in particular made no sense.

But in this situation there was no other answer to give. All he could do was believe in her return.

"Well, don't take it too dramatically, just wait here at your leisure. It's not impossible it gets settled by talking. In any case, this completes my contract with you."

"...Come back safe."

After a moment's hesitation, those were the words Daiki chose to see her off with.

Oki let out a small wry smile, then nodded and gave her reply.

"Mm. —I'm off."

With that, Oki headed into the depths beyond the torii.

And the instant she passed under it, Oki's figure **vanished** like smoke.

"She's gone into... the barrier, I suppose."

The severance of inside from outside. He'd seen similar sights in the other world.

He didn't remember seeing much of the kind an individual practitioner would use, but there'd be common ground.

The act of seeing someone off was something he doubted he'd ever get used to.

In the other world, Daiki had more often been the one being seen off.

So he'd offered the same words used to see *him* off, but waiting made him anxious all the same.

"To think I'd finally learn how the ones left behind feel, this late..."

Daiki murmured it quietly, self-mockingly.

Everything about it was too late.

Daiki Kurosu's other-world story had long since brought down its curtain.

Regret and lingering attachment would serve no purpose. In that case, the rational judgement was surely to hold only the achieved result in his chest. It was a consolation, at least.

And to wish for the power to fight here was greed of the most extravagant kind.

Having no such thing was the premise from the start; having once possessed the experience was the abnormality.

In a battle between magi, there existed no room whatsoever for Daiki Kurosu to intervene—

"—You could've gone and watched, y'know. Not a greedy one, are you. Bit surprising."

Suddenly. At the voice that rang out behind him, a shudder ran through Daiki.

He whipped around.

Daiki, who had detected even Oki's surprise attack, had been utterly unable to notice anything until he was spoken to. It was abnormal.

And there shouldn't have been any human presence inside this barrier in the first place—

"...Who are you?"

Daiki said it flatly, without any particular force.

Daiki's personality after his time in the other world was consistently calm, without swells.

However startled he was.

And indeed, when he turned — on the approach the two of them had walked up only moments before — a woman now stood.

No, girl rather than woman, perhaps.

His own age, or a little older.

Below, a denim trouser look, damaged in places.

Above, a thin patterned sweater in hard-to-describe warm tones, worn loose and baggy.

By Daiki's arbitrary impression, "art student" was the closest fit.

She wasn't very tall. Somewhere between Daiki and Oki.

Her hair was slightly bleached, and her face wore a loose, slack sort of smile that made a person's strength leak away.

Someone it was nearly impossible to be wary of — the impression, in fact, of an ordinary civilian who'd wandered in.

"Ha ha, I'd rather you didn't glare like that. Being glared at by a boy is scary stuff."

She waved a hand loosely as she said it.

Naturally, Daiki gave more weight to the fact that he hadn't been able to notice her arrival than to the impression she made.

"I'll ask again. Who are you? —Should I take it you're Oki's enemy?"

That single point was what mattered.

The intruder was supposed to be a male magus, and this wasn't at a level where disguise could be suspected. Unquestionably a different person. Unless magi could transform, in which case who knew.

Asking for information was in large part a way of buying time. Daiki was not so optimistic about the world as to think an unknown ally had suddenly appeared in this situation.

Even so — what to do.

Oki had only just gone inside the barrier.

However favourably he estimated the fastest case, he had to assume she wouldn't be back for at least ten minutes.

Should he flee?

Against the woman in front of him, was that even possible for him?

"No, no, an enemy? Hardly, hardly. Picking a fight with a scary witch is way beyond me. If anything I'm impressed you can stand being around a monster like that."

She thrust her hands into her pockets and jeered it with a shrug.

He had not the slightest intention of trusting her, but her flat declaration that she wasn't an enemy was a concern of its own.

"...Someone who shows up in this situation and knows about Oki isn't an enemy?"

"Ah, well, everyone's got their position, y'know. I'm here on a job too. That witch lady's the same, right? Though having a witch turn out is bad luck, that—"

"—Then don't call Oki a witch."

"..."

At the words Daiki suddenly came out with, the woman in front of him went round-eyed.

But it was something that needed saying, Daiki thought.

"She said she doesn't want to be called that. Only enemies do what a person hates."

"...Pfft, khh— ahahaha!"

At Daiki's deadly serious words, the woman promptly doubled over laughing.

He was being laughed at with enormous amusement, and yet — why was it? It didn't feel unpleasant.

"Y-you're saying that seriously, in this situation? Ahaha! Kid, you're a seriously good guy, aren't you! What is that, too funny... ahahaha!"

"...Is it that funny?"

"Ahaha... no, no, my apologies. I'd be glad if you didn't take offence. I was just a bit impressed, is all — onee-san here was. I could get to like you."

"Is that so. Then I'd appreciate it if you'd finally tell me who you are."

"I'm in the humble haulage business. A courier — I take what I'm asked to take to where I'm asked to take it. The name's Aiko — Urei Aiko. Aiko's the family name, mind."

"—Daiki Kurosu."

"Ahaha, very polite of you. Kid, you really are funny. Conscientious, sort of."

The woman — Urei — was laughing, chuckling away happily.

It was an exchange that drained the tension out of things.

For just an instant, enough to make him think this might pass without incident.

But.

That was surely wishful thinking.

Urei said:

"In light of which, let me tell you one more thing, nice and clear."

In that instant — Daiki felt something.

Something he couldn't put into words, akin to intuition, yet unmistakably the sense that something had changed.

The source of it was, needless to say, the woman in front of him — Urei Aiko.

"—Would it make you happier, onii-san, if I introduced myself as a magus?"

That she was by no means an existence he could relax around, Daiki now understood painfully well.

1-10 "The Former Hero, and the Magus" — 2
-------------------

"I heard only one magus had entered this city..."

"Just entering's free, don't you think? I'm strictly here on a job."

Were those words born of composure, or were they simply common sense in the magus trade?

Daiki couldn't tell how to take it.

At present Daiki had no yardstick for measuring the power of these things called magi other than Oki. It shifted depending on how strong Oki was, which was to say he might as well know nothing at all.

By her own account she was strong.

Whether or not he believed that, the magus in front of him — Urei Aiko, who evidently knew Oki — had also said things that valued that strength.

On the other hand, magi, unlike the mages of the other world Daiki had been in, apparently didn't place any particular weight on the act of combat. Certainly it wasn't an ability prioritised in modern Japan.

Taking it all together, if the ability Daiki had seen from Oki was the average—

—Then probably, merely escaping wouldn't be difficult. With conditions.

That was Daiki's judgement.

It was a given that an actual fight would be out of the question, but on the stage of flight rather than fight, finding a ray of light was by no means impossible.

At the very least, the magus in front of him showed no sign of physical training.

Her baggy clothes made the lines of her body hard to read, but however pessimistically he estimated it, she wasn't going to exceed Daiki in raw physical stamina.

Of course, he was accounting for the possibility that she might use some magecraft to boost her physical ability, as Oki had presumably done in their first encounter.

But even factoring that in, having high physical ability and being skilled at operating a body were entirely different things. Daiki had the advantage there.

So if there was a problem, it was the unknown nature of the concept of magecraft.

Even if he was faster on his feet, it meant nothing if she could, say, use a barrier to make a space you couldn't enter or leave.

And that was exactly what Daiki couldn't predict.

—Above all.

"Why did you show your face to me?"

Daiki asked it straight.

Whether he believed any answer was a separate matter. But what she chose to say was itself material for consideration.

"Never mind me — even Oki completely failed to notice you were there. You could have ambushed me if you'd wanted. Is showing yourself the composure of someone who doesn't consider me a threat?"

Daiki didn't rate the odds of that being confirmed as low. If she was a magus, she surely wouldn't recognise Daiki — a mere ordinary person — as any threat.

Even so, if a different answer came back—

Urei looked at Daiki as he tried to draw information out of her, and said:

"—By the way, kid. Daiki-kun. You're a high-schooler, right? What year?"

The reply that came back was far beyond what he'd imagined.

Slightly taken aback, Daiki answered.

"...Second year."

"Ooh, younger. Excellent. Ah, I'm eighteen this year. If I were at school I'd be a third-year!"

"...By age I'm seventeen."

"Ehh, no thanks. I'd like you to be younger there. Different school year, so what's the harm."

"What are we even talking about..."

"Forms of address, obviously! 'You' is depressing, y'know."

"..."

"So how about, with all due affection, you call me «Aiko-nee»?"

What on earth were they discussing.

She was older, yet had apparently no intention of dropping that thoroughly broken-down, odd polite speech.

After thinking a moment, Daiki put what occurred to him into words.

"...Is there magecraft you can activate on condition of making someone use a particular form of address?"

"Huh... Not to put too fine a point on it, but that's quick thinking for an amateur."

Urei's eyes went round as if impressed.

"So there is."

"Let me be clear, it isn't like I was trying to put one on you just now. That's reading too much into it. But there are plenty of conditionally triggered spells like that, sure."

"Then I'll refrain from calling you that. ...Urei."

"Ahaha, my given name! Getting called by my given name by a boy is a rarity, so honestly my heart's fluttering. Really it's just that there aren't any youngsters in this trade, so I was envious. Well, the given name is precious in its own way and gave me a pang, so we're fine this time!"

Urei stuck out her tongue and gave him a thumbs-up.

In truth it was just that her surname Aiko sounded more like a given name and was awkward to use.

Never mind.

Daiki shook his head slightly and asked again.

"So, are you not going to answer my earlier question?"

"Mm, why I showed my face? It's nothing much. I mean, obviously I'm curious. That witch — pardon, that Narumi girl — is dragging an ordinary civilian around with her. That's got to be odd. You'd think something's up. —That's why."

"...That's all?"

"That's all. I just wanted to have a little chat with you. Heh, saying it out loud is a bit embarrassing."

She was someone whose expressions changed by the second. Oki was like that too, but of a different type.

More than that, though, there was something in what she'd just said that snagged.

—Does she not know I came back from another world...?

That was the point.

Well, of course she didn't, thinking about it normally. Oki was the only one who knew.

But the possibility that the enemy magus had grasped it was something he and Oki had accounted for. That was the snag.

Which meant either the enemy magus didn't know of Daiki's abnormality, or else—

—Urei Aiko had not been given that information.

"You said you came on a job. What job, exactly?"

Daiki asked while thinking it through, and Urei nodded.

"Didn't I say? I'm in haulage. Assistance with movement. The job of conveying a client to wherever they want to go, safely and securely without anyone finding them, and cheaply into the bargain. Ah, deferred payment is negotiable, just so you know. Want a business card?"

"————"

He felt like he was starting to see it.

The client she referred to was obviously Kūya Watarai, presumably up ahead.

Concealing him from Oki's search while moving him safely about the city — she'd taken on that assistance as a job.

The grounds for believing it were, at present, that a conversation was taking place at all. That she had no intention of attacking.

"So... you yourself aren't directly party to this Kūya Watarai's plan?"

"I never said the client *was* Kūya Watarai, but... well, I might as well have. That's right. And anyway, no magus is ever going to share their own research with anyone else."

"Then... you've no intention of harming me either?"

"That's what I've been saying from the start! That's magus discrimination, that is. Granted I did introduce myself ominously. But treating someone as dangerous just for being a magus is harsh."

The odds of a lie were... low, Daiki thought.

Fundamentally, there was no benefit to Urei in deceiving him with words.

"Right, now it's my turn to ask!"

As though she'd said her piece, Urei declared it.

What to do. Daiki was still undecided about how to act, but for now Urei was not visibly dangerous.

If he believed Oki was coming back, talking was not a bad choice.

"...If it's something I can answer."

At that, Urei showed him a smile.

"Naturally I want to know about *you*! What are you? There's no way you're an ordinary civilian. And yet your background is utterly ordinary..."

"—You looked into me?"

"Nothing so grand as looking into you, and there isn't enough information to look into in the first place, is there. A high-schooler born to a perfectly unremarkable ordinary family... and yet here you are acting with a magus. What's the story? If you'd be so kind, tell onee-san."

Even asked that, Daiki thought.

"...I'm a perfectly ordinary high-school student."

"Ehh, that's not on, that's cheating! Ah, then tell me if I guess it! Hmm... the obvious first thought is a natural-born Empowered."

"..."

"Though would Narumi Oki cart someone around for that? Clearly you're not a magus. She doesn't look the type to take an apprentice, and I don't sense magical energy... You're not «Gakkai»-affiliated, are you?"

"...Sorry, I don't even know what I'm being asked."

"Argh! What *are* you, honestly, I don't get it at all! So the answer's got to be an Empowered who happened to spring up out of the ordinary populace, right? A super-powerful one at that! —How about it?!"

Even so.

Daiki answered.

"I don't really follow, but it's a problem if you want some special ability from me. I can't do anything."

"You don't have to hide it that much! You can do something, can't you? Shoot beams from your hands, or stop time, or do an astral projection and peep into the women's bath all you like!"

"...If I had to say something, I think I'm on the faster side of my class at running."

"What *is* this person?!"

Apparently he'd failed to live up to expectations somehow, and Daiki felt slightly apologetic.

No, there ought to be no need for him to feel apologetic. Even so, Daiki possessed not a single one of the bizarre abilities Urei was hoping for.

All he had was the memory of having once gone to another world.

"Haah... So you really are just an ordinary civilian? Meaning, what, that you're simply a witch's favourite...? Which is its own kind of surprise."

"...I told you, don't call Oki a—"

"**Narumi Oki is a witch.**"

At Daiki trying again to stop her using the word, Urei said it this time with force.

Daiki was slightly taken aback.

He hadn't expected to be argued with in return there.

Urei put a hand to the back of her neck, let out a small sigh, and turned her gaze back on Daiki.

"Narumi Oki is a witch, whatever she herself says. This is a simple fact."

"A simple... fact?"

"A witch and a magus are different things. A magus isn't called a witch merely for being female — just as I'm not a witch, for instance. There's a clear reason for it."

"...,"

"Playing the busybody isn't my style, and I've no obligation here. But if you really are just an ordinary civilian who got marked — you shouldn't have gone near Narumi Oki."

"...What do you mean?"

"Exactly what the words say. Well, it's too late for that anyway... yeah, meddling that doesn't pay really isn't for me. Too late. Won't make it in time. One shouldn't do things that don't suit one."

Urei let out a breath, self-mockingly.

There was a different shade to her expression than before.

At Daiki narrowing his eyes — Urei said, quietly:

"Kid, go home. Forget everything up to today and go back to a normal life."

Those were words he hadn't anticipated.

Daiki narrowed his eyes. At the very least, Urei seemed to be concerned for him.

"I've no obligation whatsoever. But well, you seem like a good sort. And if my conscience runs to stopping that sort of person from being eaten by a witch... well, mine wouldn't fetch a single yen as merchandise. Which is exactly why I'll spend it carelessly right here."

"...You're worried about me?"

"Like I said before, I'm here on a job. Transport work. I've no intention of getting involved in anything beyond that. It's not charity. So I won't do anything to you. But if the client comes back here and tries to silence you — I won't stop that either."

"..."

"You're under a misapprehension. You shouldn't demand proper ethics from a magus. And when it comes to a witch, you shouldn't even think of them as human. —That's how it is."

She was surely saying something true.

So Daiki thought. It wasn't intuition.

It was a wish — the wish that it were so.

"...You're a good person, Urei."

So Daiki said. Urei waved a hand with genuine distaste.

"Oh, come on. It's because you're the sort who'd think that here that I'm saying it... Were you not listening properly? I told you I'd abandon you even if you got killed."

"No, I'm saying it having listened."

"Then you're an idiot, kid. —That sort dies."

Urei's words rang cold.

In fact, if he ignored this warning she really would abandon him. It was an ultimatum.

"...I know that."

Knowing it, that was why Daiki shaped his words.

"It's usually the kind ones who die first. It's the same in any world — I know that well enough to be sick of it."

"...What are you—"

"It's a mistake to call me a good person. If I really were one, there's no way I'd be the only one alive. But you see — I'm done with all that."

In the other world, Daiki had been far too strong.

Among the creatures called human, he may well have been the strongest.

Turn that over, and it means he had watched those weaker than himself go on ahead, over and over.

That he had killed enemies weaker than himself, again and again.

Because it had been necessary.

Because it was asked of him. Because he wanted to go home.

He was needed and he had needs, so there had been no room for choice. —And all of that he had left behind in a place he could never reach again.

—Surely that was enough.

So Daiki thought. He had duly returned to his former life.

In that case, what was the harm in pursuing something he hadn't been able to do before?

Let him have at least that much.

"Thanks for the warning. But I'm going to wait for Oki here."

He surely knew nothing about Narumi Oki. Theirs was a relationship one day old.

Urei, standing before him, probably knew far more about Oki than he did.

What she was saying was surely correct. Even so.

—They had made a contract.

She had asked him: help me, and I'll help you.

Even if he couldn't be of much use, even if it had been a pretext for the contract — even so, she had helped Daiki that night, and if he could repay her for it, that was enough.

"Oki will come back. So I'm—"

"—Like I said. **It's too late.**"

Urei Aiko said it with a transparent expression.

No emotion visible whatsoever — those were a magus's eyes.

She had certainly given Daiki a warning.

But exactly as she said, the notion that this made Urei Aiko a kind person was entirely off the mark.

Because she was a magus.

"Narumi Oki isn't coming back. She'll certainly be killed up ahead — no, she's already dead."

"...What did you say?"

Daiki's eyes went wide.

To him, Urei spoke as though relating plain fact.

"Narumi Oki is strong, certainly. She's called a witch for a reason. But in a fight between magi there's no such thing as 'absolutely.' —Which is exactly why Narumi Oki will certainly be killed."

The words sounded contradictory. But he could feel their weight.

At the very least, Urei was stating the facts as she understood them.

"What's up ahead is a stage set for killing a witch. A special stage prepared for that purpose. The moment she stepped onto it, Narumi Oki was already in checkmate. She won't come back alive."

"...So there *was* a trap."

"She walked into it knowing, so there's no room for sympathy. That's why I told you to go home."

Urei Aiko held no sympathy for a witch.

So that pity was directed not at the witch but at the young man dragged into it.

—And because he understood that, Daiki said:

"I see, understood. Thank you for telling me. —I'm going in."

There was no hesitation; the decision took an instant.

At the switch, it was Urei who was taken aback.

"—Huh...? What... are you saying, kid..."

"At this rate Oki gets killed, right? Then obviously I'm going to save her."

"...Is your head broken?"

"If you ask me, abandoning her is the insane option."

"..."

Urei could tell he was serious.

Which was why she didn't understand. Had he been a fool who grasped neither the disparity in power nor the threat of a magus, she could have understood.

But he wasn't stupid enough to miss that. He had never once lowered his guard against her, a magus.

That was the attitude of someone who understood what a magus was.

"...I won't let you."

And so Urei Aiko said it.

She had no choice but to say it.

"You're going to stop me?"

At Daiki turning to ask, Urei shook her head.

"**I simply have to stop you.** —That's my job."

"..."

"Run and that's fine. Chasing isn't my job. Wait, that's fine too. Silencing and vengeance are outside my remit. But entering I can't allow. —Holding you up is the job I took."

"...Weren't you a courier?"

"Which is what I'm doing. Conveying anyone who tries to go in, back to the outside."

She wasn't going to let him past.

She wasn't that soft an opponent. In which case, what could Daiki do?

"—!"

In that instant, Daiki broke into a run at once.

At any rate, getting past the torii — intruding into the barrier — was the priority.

In terms of position he had the overwhelming advantage; better to flee than to engage.

But — that was a method that would have worked if his opponent were not a magus.

"—Gh, wha—"

His feet — wouldn't move.

As though the soles of his shoes were glued to the ground, they wouldn't come free at all.

"When did she—!"

"—That kind of thinking is exactly not understanding what a magus is."

Before he knew it, Urei was standing right in front of him.

—Merely escaping is possible, he'd thought; far too naive.

Daiki had no choice but to feel it keenly. Magi were far beyond the range of his comprehension.

"Gh, wai—"

"—That's what you get for thinking you can take it lightly."

And then.

Urei's right hand was swung at Daiki.

1-11 "The Former Hero, and the Magus" — 3
